| D15 (retire STIX heuristic) | **done** | distribution-engineer + schema-architect | ws6/d15-retire-stix-heuristic (merged `378bf9b3`, pushed) | **✅ PASS (red-reviewer, 2026-07-29, BOUNCE COUNT ZERO, all 9 checks reached) — MERGED per D7.** **Proof at the artifact level, not argued from set equality:** the reviewer loaded **both** exporter versions into one process — merge-base via `git show a67acebc:scripts/export_stix.py` exec'd into its own namespace, HEAD from disk — and built both bundles from **the same in-memory corpus object**, eliminating the regenerate-against-a-moved-corpus failure mode entirely. Result **byte-identical**: sha256 `a242af68f7e0f471…` both sides, 60,457,177 bytes, 54,099 objects, `x_content_license` on **1,517 of 13,119** before and after, **0 under-attribution, 0 over-attribution**. **Sequencing verified rather than assumed:** run against the **pre-E16** corpus (`cbfc57eb^`), the heuristic caught **95 rows the field missed** — retiring then would have stripped exactly those. E16 extending the marker to all 95 is what made retirement safe, and this branch touches no data at all. **A2 — no fallback kept — CONFIRMED, on a stronger ground than the implementation offered.** The counter-argument assumes the fallback is a safety net. **It is not.** `content_license` feeds five surfaces; a row that silently loses the field loses its marker on `data/incidents.json`, all three `incidents.min.json` mirrors **and** the HF export regardless — the fallback lived **only** on STIX. So the trade was never "visible failure vs safety net"; it was "visible failure vs **concealed** failure already happening on four other surfaces." The fallback would have held the STIX count at a reassuring 1,517 while the corpus rotted — **under-attribution laundered into invisibility**, which is precisely the direction D15 names unsafe. **Two supporting arguments worth keeping as general principles:** (i) the heuristic structurally contradicts the field's own stated design (schema: "source-generic by design — the source is named in the `source` VALUE, never in the field name"), and AIAAIC-hardcoded substring matching over `tags`/`source_ids` cannot serve a second source and would false-positive on any future tag containing the string; (ii) **a fallback that fires only when the primary path fails is an untested path by construction — it would be exercised for the first time during the incident it was meant to cover.** **`DATA_DICTIONARY.md:70` needs no re-hedge and the claim got STRONGER:** pre-D15 "an exact match, not a heuristic-inflated count" was true only **empirically** (the sets happened to coincide after E16); post-D15 it is true **structurally**, because `_content_license()` is a one-line passthrough. **Checklist re-scope is a CORRECTION, not a weakening:** old `description_source`-keyed query returns **95**, re-scoped `content_license.source`-keyed returns **0**; all 95 have **no** `description_source` at all and every one declares `source == "aiaaic"` — they are exactly the E16/D18 hand-curated set. Escape-hatch language preserved verbatim. **Spec treatments judged consistent with each file's own precedent:** `marker-shape` preserves its dated 2026-07-27 measurement (1,513/13,115) and appends a dated block — correct, because it amends a *rule* in place while preserving a *dated measurement of a corpus state that genuinely existed*; `rescoped` rewrites the operative bullet plus a dated changelog note, matching the changelog directly above it. **Schema mirror regenerated and committed in the same commit — the E16 bounce-#1 defect did NOT recur.** Parsed-JSON comparison: 316 leaf paths both sides, **0 added, 0 removed, exactly 1 changed**; `if`/`then` byte-identical; key order preserved. Tests 227→226 (2 heuristic-only dropped, 1 added); the new test proven **non-vacuous by execution** — its fixture emits a marker under BASE and none under HEAD. Build fixed-point clean across two full runs; `stix` confirmed not a `make build` prerequisite and its outputs gitignored. Strays clean, invariants clean, `validate.py` 13,119/13,119. |

**All ten exit criteria met**, each backed by re-derived evidence in the gated
checklist `docs/audits/PHASE1-EXIT-2026-07-31.md` — no criterion marked met on
attestation alone. **v2.9.0 cut and tagged at `118a0141`** (tag on origin,
SHA-verified). **Zenodo version deposit `10.5281/zenodo.21729952`**, under
concept DOI `10.5281/zenodo.20248675` per E11.

**Criterion 9 clause 2 closed at 4 of 4** — the last open item, closed by the
user sending the OECD outreach on 2026-07-31. **FLAG 3 retired.**

**Four outreach threads live, clocks running:** AIAAIC **2026-08-26** · MIT
AIRI **2026-08-28** (D8's retire-or-replace decision date) · OECD **2026-08-21**
(21 days from its 2026-07-31 send) · AIID sent 2026-07-27, no clock owed.

**What shipped.** Seven gated merges: the E21 §5 OECD narrative reduction; §5.3
per-entry attribution on 3,829 of 3,829 OECD-sourced rows; the licensing
surfaces (OECD added as a third obligated source, dep5's inverted
database-right claim corrected); the README rewrite; the v2.9.0 packet; the
exit checklist; the UA honesty bump. Corpus **12,986 → 13,060** (+74 net; 61
removed, **all tombstoned, 0 silently deleted**). AIAAIC `content_license`
markers on **1,517** rows. AIID resolved — no marker owed — with a tripwire
test guarding the dormant population.

**FLAGS 2 and 5 remain disclosed, not resolved** — they are statements about
*history* (criteria that were once satisfied on false or absent dates), and
marking them "resolved" would be a small lie. **FLAG 4 remains open** with its
undecided fork: `CHANGELOG`'s one surviving "TAXII endpoint" sits under a
past-release heading, where agreement 4 says supersede and WS6-T4's criterion
says correct in place. **Decide before dispatching WS6-T4.**

**Routed to Phase 2, none blocking:** `.zenodo.json`'s unguarded version key ·
the stale `VERSION = "2.0.0"` export · the WS6-T4 fork · the OECD
partition-collapse proposal · generating `NOTICE-DATA` from a manifest ·
OECD provenance labelling · the seven-surface version inventory (WS6-T1) ·
a doc/code UA sync test.

**What this phase actually taught**, recorded because it outlives the release:
the dominant failure mode was **checks that cannot fail** — seven instances
across four agents and six unrelated tools, in three escalating forms. A check
whose output is identical whether or not it ran. A second party "confirming" a
figure by re-running the first party's method. And a claim rewritten
qualitatively until nothing could check it at all. **Removing a figure does not
remove the claim; it removes the ability to check it.** The counterweight, both
times it mattered, was someone using a *different route* — a drafter that
refused to write the board's number and cross-validated three ways, and a gate
that deliberately chose a method nobody had used.

**Phase 2 opens with the E5 / WS1-T4 scope decision**, measurement already
committed at `docs/audits/E5-corpus-composition-2026-07-31.md`. **To be taken
fresh, in a new session.**

(`MASTER_IMPROVEMENT_PLAN.md` §WS6-T1, scope + accept). Published with

**byte-identical** to `docs/releases/v2.9.0.md` (sha256
`5db3047c38a059f0d5065002c6ef0599b8d559583979991aecd2efd4cef4848b`, both sides,
18,682 chars / 338 lines — no rewriting, no new prose); annotated tag

**⚠ Two link defects in the published body — DISCLOSED, NOT FIXED, and the fix
is the user's call, not the foreman's.** Both are artifacts of publishing a
repo-relative document verbatim as a release body, and fixing either means
editing the body away from the gated text: (1) `../../README.md#how-to-cite`
renders as `/emmanuelgjr/genai_incidents/README.md#how-to-cite` → **HTTP 404**
(measured; `/blob/main/README.md` → 200 is the form that works from a release
page); (2) the in-body `#verification` and
`#consumer-impact-the-59-tombstoned-ids` anchors are **dead links** — GitHub
emits **zero** `user-content-*` heading ids in release bodies (measured: 0), so
release-body anchor links have no targets, unlike the same file rendered under
`docs/`. **Neither defect touches a figure, a claim, or a corpus row** — the
notes' content is intact and byte-verified above; only intra-document
navigation is affected. **This is a general property of the new checklist step,
not a v2.9.0 quirk:** any future notes file written to live under `docs/` will
break the same two ways when reused verbatim as a release body, so the
VERSIONING.md step should say which link forms survive the move.

## 🚨 E21 TRIPWIRE FIRED on the first real refresh in 8 weeks — 28 new AIID-signal/OECD-content rows — opened 2026-09-14 — **investigating**

**The manual `workflow_dispatch` (user-authorized) worked as a test of the persist fix.**
- Run [`34858279213`](https://github.com/emmanuelgjr/genai_incidents/actions/runs/34858279213) on `main` @ `05f536ff`. **Persist ✅** for the first time since 2026-07-19; `origin/refresh-state` moved `f41ad68e` → **`bfbdec57`** (aiaaic, oecd and kev all `ok` / last_success 2026-09-14). Re-merge + render + validate ✅.
- It then **failed at Unit tests (1 failed, 318 passed)**, a step no run had reached in 8 weeks, so **no refresh PR opened and nothing reached main.** The gate failed closed, as designed.

**What fired:** `tests/test_e21_partA_inc00437_provenance.py::test_oecd_aiid_content_disagreement_is_unique_to_inc00437`. The E21 Part A invariant says exactly one aiid_id-bearing row (1574 / INC-00437) ships a description that is not AIID's template. The refreshed inputs produce **29**: `[1552, 1574, 1584, 1604, 1610, 1612, 1616, 1622, 1641, 1642, 1643, 1644, 1646, 1647, 1650, 1654, 1656, 1658, 1659, 1660, 1661, 1662, 1665, 1666, 1668, 1669, 1671, 1673, 1674]`.

**Foreman's working hypothesis (NOT yet established; that is the investigation's job):**
- `ingest/aiid_full.json` (the D1 sanctioned AIID snapshot) holds **1,548 rows, max aiid_id 1581**. Its last commit is `19fa2986` (OWASP migration); its last *content* refresh has not been checked.
- `ingest_aiid_snapshot.py` runs from the **Makefile only, not from auto-refresh**. The weekly OECD AIM refresh therefore brings in rows cross-referencing AIID incidents newer than the snapshot. No AIID row competes for those `AIID-<n>` keys, so OECD's row wins the merge. That is INC-00437's exact mechanism, now at scale.
- **1552 < 1581 does not fit this story** and needs its own explanation.
- **Why it matters beyond a test:** these rows would ship OECD-template text under an AIID cross-reference signal. Whether that also mislabels `description_provenance` / `description_source` is the provenance question E21/E23 ruled on.

**Note for next Sunday:** the scheduled 2026-09-20 run will fail at the same place, fail-closed with no PR. That is safe, but it means the refresh stays blocked until this is resolved.

**History: attempt 1 and BOUNCE #1** (preserved as written):

**Why this exists: the corpus has been frozen at 13,060 since v2.9.0, because the weekly refresh has not opened a PR since #97.**
`auto-refresh.yml` has gone red on **9 consecutive scheduled runs, 2026-07-19 → 2026-09-13**. They are **not all the same failure**:
- **2026-07-19** (run `29676405074`) took the `--orphan` path, created `refresh-state` (`f41ad68e`), opened the PR, and failed at the **intended** "Enforce source health" step (AIRI stale).
- **The 8 runs 2026-07-26 → 2026-09-13** (e.g. `30191177187`, `34748318826`) failed at **"Persist source health counters to refresh-state branch"**: `fatal: 'origin/refresh-state' is not a commit` (exit 128).

**Mechanism:** `git clone --depth 1` implies `--single-branch`, so `git fetch origin refresh-state` writes only FETCH_HEAD and the checkout of `origin/refresh-state` dies. `set -e` then skips rebuild, tests, and the PR step. That throws away ~50 minutes of successful ingest every week. The "branch exists" path was never exercised before D5-impl merged. **Why nobody noticed:** the board had pre-announced the run would go RED by design (AIRI), so a *different* red read as the expected one. The Restore step is **not** affected: actions/checkout sets a wildcard refspec, and its logs say "Restored".

**Branch** `ws4/refresh-state-persist-fix` (pushed) · owner pipeline-engineer · attempt 1 `8debf617`: extract the step to `scripts/persist_refresh_state.sh` with an explicit `refresh-state:refs/remotes/origin/refresh-state` refspec; `tests/test_persist_refresh_state.py` (local bare-repo fixture, cases a/b/c); INGESTION_CONDUCT register repointed to `scripts/persist_refresh_state.sh:59`; `.gitattributes` `*.sh text eol=lf`. Suite 318 (314 + 4).

**─── VERDICT (agreement 5) — red-reviewer, 2026-09-14: BOUNCE #1 ───** *"The functional fix is verified correct … Both defects are committed-text fixes, so a narrow re-gate is enough."*

## 🔤 OWASP LLM TOP 10 → 2026 EDITION — user-directed, OUT OF PLAN, landed 2026-08-17, **NOT GATED**

**Not a WS task.** Direct user request during the Phase-1/Phase-2 interval, executed
by the main session rather than dispatched — so **no specialist owned it and no
red-reviewer verdict exists.** Recorded here because it changed a core taxonomy on
every published surface, and a change of that size with no board row is exactly the
archaeology this file exists to prevent.

**Branch** `taxonomy/owasp-llm-top10-2026` · **PR #99** (open, 5/5 checks green,
MERGEABLE, unmerged) · commits `19fa2986` (migration), `3f634a05` (verify gate),
`19dc8f66` (site link). Record: `docs/audits/owasp-llm-2026-migration-delta-2026-08-17.md`.

**What changed.** OWASP published the 2026 edition 2026-08-03. It is a **renumbering
plus one rename** — no entry added, none removed — so 2025→2026 is a **bijection**
over `LLM01`–`LLM10`. **17,498 code assignments across 11,556 of 13,060 entries**
rewritten. `LLM07` System Prompt Leakage → `LLM08` **Hidden Context Exposure**
(renamed and widened). Largest moves: Excessive Agency `LLM06`→`LLM03`, Improper
Output Handling `LLM05`→`LLM10`, Unbounded Consumption `LLM10`→`LLM06`.
Crosswalk: `mappings/owasp_llm_2025_to_2026.json`; 2025 catalog **retained and
marked superseded**, because every release ≤ v2.9.0 and its DOIs carry 2025 codes
and cannot be read without it.

**Sourcing note, because it nearly went wrong.** `genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/` still
serves the **2025** list, and the top-ranked "2026" secondary writeup recycles it;
a second syndicated source published an ordering that contradicted both itself and
the primary. The list was taken from the **official PDF (122 pp.) and the
`GenAI-Security-Project/GenAI-LLM-Top10` repo**, and the two agree exactly.

**⚠ This is a SILENT breaking change.** The code space is **identical before and
after**, so `schema/incident.schema.json` validates a correct migration, a partial
one, and a **doubly-applied** one equally happily. `LLM03` stays valid and changes
meaning. Nothing in CI could have caught a wrong permutation — hence the permutation
lives in data and is asserted bijective at load, the migration fails closed on any
undeclared field change, and two independent guards block double-apply.

**Evidence — [R] re-derived, but SELF-verified, not independently gated.** A control
build was made in a clean worktree at `HEAD` (pre-migration code AND inputs) and
compared entry-by-entry with the migrated build: **ID sets identical (13,060,
symmetric difference 0) · entries whose `owasp_llm` ≠ crosswalk image of the control:
0 · entries with ANY other field changed: 0 · 17,498 → 17,498.** Plus
`validate.py` 13060/13060 valid 0 errors, `check_stats_drift` clean, 314 tests.
**The measurements are [R]; the absence of a gate is the gap.** Under agreement 5
the verdict is testimony — here there is no verdict to testify to. **`docs-warden`
has NOT run** despite protocol step 7 applying (taxonomy lists changed); the
cross-surface sweep was the foreman's own and is therefore `[A]`-class on the one
question that matters, *did an independent party check this*.

**The control build earned its cost — it caught a real defect.** See PRECEDENT 4
below: retain-on-drop rows are a build **input**, an inputs-only migration missed 9
of them, **and the totals still matched exactly** while the distribution was wrong.

**Scope limit, stated so nobody later reads completeness into it: renumbered, NOT
re-classified.** Several 2026 entries widened their scope (LLM08 now covers all
non-user-facing context, not just the system prompt; LLM01 cross-modal injection;
LLM10 insecure generated code). Incidents that would **newly qualify** were **not**
added — that means re-running classifiers over descriptions, i.e. the WS0-T3 cascade
hazard. **Mappings are complete w.r.t. the 2025 labelling and conservative w.r.t.
2026 scope.** Closing that gap is WS2 annotation work, routed below.

**Open:** E24 (version ruling — the CHANGELOG entry sits under `[Unreleased]`
deliberately). **Deployment:** `pages.yml` fires only on push to `main`, so the live
site still serves 2025 until #99 merges; HuggingFace and PyPI trigger on
`release: published`, so published artifacts stay on 2025 until a release is cut.

If that token is a REAL path, the replay TRUNCATES it to zero bytes. `CLAUDE.md` carries the sentence "never hand-edit data/*.json" — any command echoing a string of that shape aimed at a real file would empty `data/incidents.json` or `PROGRESS.md`. Silent, no error, no diff until too late. **Also a permission bypass:** a DENIED command still produced a side effect, so denial is not currently preventing writes. **Trigger confirmed; COMPONENT PINNED (2026-07-17).** Root cause is the **claude-flow `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse` hook suite**, which re-parsed agent Bash command strings through `cmd /c` with quoting collapsed — a quoted `->` became a real redirect creating a zero-byte file named for the next token. The denied-command side effect fits: `PreToolUse` runs BEFORE the permission decision takes effect, so a denied command's string was already reprocessed. Removed from global settings 2026-07-17; three clean canaries (`zzcanary11/12/13`) confirmed the generator dead. **Ruled out:** the `PostToolUse` Write/Edit hook (exact backtick content reproduced via a scratchpad Write — no file appeared) and a timer (nothing appears absent a triggering command). Retro-explains the original three (`1457`, `truly`, `` ` ``, 2026-07-15 23:30–00:04): tokens from earlier sessions' quoted command strings, NOT anything the user typed. | 2026-07-16, during the D6 fix | **RESOLVED 2026-07-17** — claude-flow `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse` hooks removed from global settings; generator confirmed dead by three clean canaries. Mitigation RETAINED as insurance against any future command-reprocessing layer: agents keep redirect characters and backticks out of quoted strings in Bash commands (red-reviewer step 5c). **This is why D6 was softened** — the generator fired on the reviewer's own evidence commands, so strays could not be attributed to the task under review; the softened attribution rule stays. |

## Escalations — NEW 2026-08-17 (E24) — the OWASP 2026 migration is a breaking change nothing in CI can detect, and it needs a version ruling

| ID | Finding | Source | Status |
|----|---------|--------|--------|
| E24 | **`owasp_llm` now carries OWASP LLM Top 10 **2026** codes (PR #99). The code space is unchanged (`LLM01`–`LLM10`), so this is a semantic break with no syntactic signal: `LLM03` remains a valid code and now means *Excessive Agency* instead of *Supply Chain*.** Every consumer filtering on literal codes silently changes meaning — no error, no schema violation, no CI failure, and **no way for a downstream reader to detect which edition a given file uses except by the release it came from.** Data at or before **v2.9.0** (concept DOI `10.5281/zenodo.20248675`, version deposit `10.5281/zenodo.21729952`) carries 2025 codes. Mitigations shipped: crosswalk `mappings/owasp_llm_2025_to_2026.json`, retained 2025 catalog, and a compatibility note in `docs/TAXONOMIES.md`. **Needs from the user: the version number.** D19 ruled v2.9.0 by simple increment for Phase-1; that lever is not available here — a semantic break argues **major**, but `v3.0.0` is committed to Phase 3 (and `v3.0.0-beta` to Phase 2) as a *breaking-change* commitment, so this collides with the plan's reserved numbers exactly the way D19's stale `v2.7.0` target collided with history. Options the foreman will not choose unilaterally: (a) minor `v2.10.0` and treat the code-space stability as "not an API break" — **defensible only if the release notes are loud, since the break is real for data consumers even though the schema is unchanged**; (b) major `v3.0.0` now, forcing Phase 2/3 to renumber; (c) hold the release until Phase 2 opens and ship the renumbering inside `v3.0.0-beta`, which is where the plan already expects consumer-visible breakage. **Until ruled: CHANGELOG sits under `[Unreleased]`, no bump, deliberately — same discipline as D19, cut once and correctly.** | Foreman, 2026-08-17, from the migration landed above | **OPEN — needs a user ruling.** Not blocking the #99 merge (merging deploys the site; it does not publish a release). Blocks HuggingFace and PyPI, which trigger on `release: published`. |

The fallback has nothing to fall back for. **(2) The third-party-news premise does not survive contact with the data.** On a deep-parsed page (`2026-06-30-7d4d`), `summary` appears **nowhere** in the concatenated evidences blob (6 strings, 3,664 chars) — not whole, not sentence by sentence. It is a **composed OECD abstract**. And AIM's `evidences` are not quoted news copy either — they read as OECD's own harm assessments (*"The AI system's role in generating and spreading false content is pivotal to the harm experienced"*). **So the text shipping in all 3,667 rows is OECD-COMPOSED EDITORIAL, not third-party article prose** — which against the board's CC-BY-4.0-as-of-2024-07-01 record means §1.5's `Redistribute-verbatim: NO` and the escalation row at `:343` describe **a code path that produced nothing.** **Consequence for the user's three questions, which this reorders:** the §1.5 re-analysis should come **first**, because it may establish there is nothing to reduce for compliance reasons; the reduction may still be worth doing as **scope hygiene** (a 337-char abstract is not a structural fact), but that is a scope decision, not a licensing one, and **if the licensing rationale is void then E21 is NOT a Phase-1 exit blocker.** Caveat the gate itself flagged: no CC notice appears on the methodology page, so the CC BY 4.0 fact still rests on the board's 2026-07-16 record rather than on that page. **Do not rebuild 3,667 descriptions before the licensing question is settled.** |
| E22 | **A 302 redirect defeats invariant 5's robots.txt and rate-limit guarantees — on the weekly path, on the day the invariant activated.** Flagged by license-auditor from a code read; **foreman confirmed live and end-to-end.** `scripts/ingest_oecd_aim.py:38`'s `SITEMAP_URL = https://oecd.ai/sitemaps/incident-monitor-sitemap.xml` returns **`302 → https://incidents-server.oecdai.org/api/v1/sitemap.xml` — a different host** (measured 2026-07-30; `urllib` auto-follows, and the followed final URL's netloc differs from `oecd.ai`). Meanwhile `ingest/common.py` derives the rate-limit host at `:364` from `urlparse(url).netloc` — the **pre-redirect** URL — and `robots_allowed(url)` at `:280` takes the same pre-redirect URL. **A grep for any post-response `resp.url`/`geturl()` re-check returns ZERO hits: nothing anywhere reconciles the host we checked against the host we actually fetched.** So the bytes arrive from a host whose robots.txt is never fetched and whose rate limit is never applied, while the pipeline records conduct compliance against a host it only *intended* to contact. **This is worse than the CISA case that earned an enumerated allowlist:** CISA's unverifiable robots.txt fails CLOSED and is documented; a redirect fails **silently open**, and by the same mechanism an allowlisted host could redirect anywhere. **Bounded today, and the foreman measured the bound:** of 8 real ingest targets probed for first-hop behaviour, **the OECD sitemap is the ONLY redirect** — CISA KEV, CAPEC, AIID snapshots, NVD API and `docs.google.com` all return 200 with no redirect (AIRI zip 404s per WS4-T9/E6, and `oecd.ai/en/incidents/<id>` returned 404 on the sampled ID so per-page redirect behaviour is still unconfirmed — the auditor could not settle it with WebFetch either). **So: one live instance, on a weekly-scheduled ingest, in the module whose entire purpose is that guarantee.** Fix shape (pipeline-engineer, WS4): re-check robots and re-apply pacing against the **post-redirect** host, or refuse to follow cross-host redirects without an explicit re-check — and state which in `docs/INGESTION_CONDUCT.md`, since §2's fail-closed claim currently reads broader than the code delivers. **Not a defect in the merged gate's terms** (no acceptance criterion or reviewed claim covered redirects, and neither gate brief raised them), but it is a real gap in an ACTIVE invariant and the conduct doc overstates coverage until it is fixed or disclosed. | license-auditor flag; foreman-confirmed live, 2026-07-30 | ⚠ **OPEN.** Recommend folding into the **WS4 cache-write class audit** already opened in `ce223784` — same owner, same "the guarantee has a hole the tests do not see" shape — or a P1 standalone if that audit is not next. `docs/INGESTION_CONDUCT.md` needs the disclosure either way. **✅ BLAST RADIUS SETTLED 2026-07-30 by the gate — 1 fetch per weekly run, NOT 10,000.** Individual `/en/incidents/<id>` pages do **not** redirect: **7 probes all `200` with `REDIRECTS=0`** (3 IDs from the ingest artifact, 4 fresh from the live sitemap — `2020-01-01-39b5`, `2020-01-02-38b6`, `2020-01-02-96f4`, `2026-06-30-7d4d`, `2026-07-22-01d5`, `2026-06-22-8ea6`, `2026-06-28-ab9b`). **The foreman's earlier 404 was a bad ID, not evidence of anything** — the auditor's hedge that this was "a shell-level question WebFetch could not resolve" was the right call, and the answer is no. Also verified: the redirected sitemap still emits **10,001 `<loc>` entries, 10,000 matching the script's `oecd\.ai/en/incidents/[^/]+$` filter, zero on the new host** — so there is no silent-zero-URL failure lurking behind the redirect. Code side re-confirmed independently: `ingest/common.py:364` `host = urlparse(url).netloc` → `:365` `_rate_limit(host, ...)`, `robots_allowed()` at `:280` on the same pre-redirect URL, and grep for `resp.url`/`geturl()`/`redirect` in the module returns **zero hits**. **Fix shape (gate advisory 8, WS4 pipeline-engineer):** compare `resp.url`/`resp.geturl()` against the pre-redirect netloc and re-run `robots_allowed()` + `_rate_limit()` on the effective host, or refuse cross-host redirects unless allowlisted. **Also owed (advisory 9): once E22 has a task ID, §1.5's Scrape-permitted cell — currently the only place recording the redirect — should point at that ID** rather than at "the check named in the report for red-reviewer", which will not survive as a durable reference. |

## Escalations — NEW 2026-07-29 (E15)
| # | Item | Raised | Status |
|---|------|--------|--------|
| E15 | **Does the retained AIAAIC headline defeat D2's "copyright share-alike question is disposed of" conclusion?** Surfaced by license-auditor while fixing the deleted-disclosure regression, and it touches **D2 itself**, so it is a user call rather than a rewording. **The fact:** `ingest_aiaaic_sheet.py:535` carries AIAAIC's headline verbatim as the entry `title` on all 1,422 AIAAIC-derived rows. D2, `SOURCE_LICENSES.md` §1.1, NOTICE-DATA and `.reuse/dep5` all conclude the facts-only reduction "disposes of the *copyright* share-alike question". **The auditor's read: that is more confident than UK/EU law supports.** Bare facts are categorically uncopyrightable (*Feist*; equivalent elsewhere) — which is what an "only categorical facts" premise would safely support — but a headline is an editorial characterisation, not a bare fact. US doctrine (Copyright Office Circular 33) *does* categorically exclude "titles, names, short phrases and slogans", favouring de minimis; **UK/EU doctrine does NOT.** *Infopaq* (CJEU C-5/08, 2009) held an 11-word extract can be protected where it reflects "the author's own intellectual creation" through choice and combination of words, and *NLA v Meltwater* ([2010] EWHC 3099 (Ch), aff'd [2011] EWCA Civ 890) held newspaper headlines specifically CAN stand as independent copyright works, assessed case by case — no blanket exemption. Sample retained headlines embed quoted editorial colour ("accused of 'unprecedented web scraping'", "puts young Londoners in 'serious danger'") — the compositional choice *Infopaq*/*Meltwater* treat as pushing TOWARD protection. **AIAAIC is UK-situated and its CC BY-SA 4.0 grant is the operative one, and E13 already treats UK/EU as the governing regime, so the permissive US short-phrase doctrine is not ours to default to.** **The practical saving grace: no new engineering either way** — the row-level `content_license` marker built for the database-right hedge is ALSO the correct remedy if the headline needs BY-SA, and it already runs on every AIAAIC row. If this resolves against de minimis, the fix is prose only: soften "disposes of the copyright share-alike question" to something like "disposes of the copyright question for AIAAIC's *narrative prose* (never carried); the retained headline is treated as BY-SA-encumbered and honored via the same row-level marker" — and it must change in `SOURCE_LICENSES.md` §1.1, NOTICE-DATA and dep5 TOGETHER so the consumer surfaces do not re-diverge from the master doc, which is how the last regression happened. **Interim posture already shipped (`38b7c99f`), and it hedges correctly without prejudging this:** NOTICE-DATA now states the retained headline "is itself AIAAIC-authored text, not a bare fact" and is covered by the row-level BY-SA marker on every AIAAIC-derived row — so the surfaces disclose the exposure and honor it even while the doctrinal question is open. **Options for the user:** (a) fold into the E13 counsel question as a joint ask — both hinge on the same "how protective is UK/EU law over AIAAIC's contribution" analysis, and E13 already carries a counsel posture; (b) soften the prose now on the auditor's read without counsel, since the remedy is already implemented and costs nothing; (c) accept the current hedged wording as sufficient and revisit only if AIAAIC's reply (clock 2026-08-26) speaks to it. | license-auditor, during the disclosure fix, 2026-07-29 | **DECIDED 2026-07-29 → D17: option (a), fold into the E13 counsel ask as a joint question.** Not blocking: the disclosure fix stands on its own. |

**Foreman process note 2026-07-27 (dual-dispatch incident, no damage):** during session-resume reconstruction the foreman judged the link-1 schema-architect dead (branch had no commits, one uncommitted file) and dispatched a completion-pass instance — then the original completed and committed (`22cfcd23`) while the second was verifying. Two schema-architect instances were briefly live on one branch, violating the sole-writer rule in fact. No clobber occurred (the second re-verified the committed bytes match what it tested, then committed only a memo amendment `d1351717`); both instances now stood down; the branch is frozen pending the gate. Lesson: an uncommitted tree plus a silent agent is not proof of death — check for the agent before redispatching onto the same files, and prefer a stand-down handshake first.

A COUNT COPIED IS A COUNT THAT DRIFTS."** **(a) DATASHEET takes option 1** — describe the obligation **qualitatively** and **point** to `docs/SOURCE_LICENSES.md` §1.5 / `NOTICE-DATA`, which already carry the exact counts. ## WS2 / WS1 inputs — from the WS0-T3 cascade audit (2026-07-18)
- **(iii) AIRI ingestion is LOSSY on causal intent — 708 of 1,380 AIRI-derived rows retain no intent information at all (2026-07-29, user-directed board line).** `scripts/ingest_airi_navigator.py:126-135` treats AIRI's three classification cells asymmetrically: `eu_ai_act_risk_level` and `causal_timing` are **value-preserving** (every row yields a tag, so both cover all 1,380), but `causal_intent` is a **filter** — `if row.get("causal_intent") == "Intentional"` — so only that single value survives ingestion. Measured: **672 of 1,380** rows carry `intentional`; for the other **708**, AIRI's intent classification is discarded and nothing about it is retained. **This is a lossy-ingestion fact about the corpus, not a wording bug.** Surfaced as a defect in an outward-facing draft (E18 bounce #2, where the message implied intent coverage on all 1,380), but the underlying finding is independent of that email and outlives it. **Two Phase-3 consumers need it: WS2-T3** (per-mapping `{method, confidence}` — an absent tag here means "not retained," NOT "not intentional," and any confidence model that reads absence as a negative signal will be wrong on 708 rows) **and the DATASHEET composition section** (a documented selective-retention decision that shapes what the corpus can answer). **Same family as (ii)**, the AIAAIC editorial-vocabulary finding: in both cases one upstream source's ingestion choices silently shape a published distribution. Logged now so it survives to Phase 3 rather than being rediscovered.
Logged for the (not-yet-open) Phase-2 label-quality and corpus-bias tasks. Source: `docs/audits/WS0-T3-cascade-2026-07-18.md`.
- **(i) Source-derived taxonomy population (~88 entries).** The ~88 AIAAIC entries whose `LLM09` (+ cascaded `mitre_atlas` `AML.T0058` / `nist_ai_rmf` `MEASURE-2.8`) mappings were mechanically seeded from AIAAIC's `ethical` cell (the "Mis/disinformation" string) are **not** independent mapping judgments. **WS2-T3** must tag these `method: source-derived`; **WS2-T2** (two-annotator Cohen-κ study) must **stratify** on them separately from independently-mapped entries.
- **(ii) AIAAIC editorial vocabulary materially shapes the `misinformation` attack_vector distribution.** 216 AIAAIC entries' `attack_vector = misinformation` hinge on a single `disinform` token in AIAAIC's `ethical` cell. This is a **WS1-T6** source-bias signal (one source's editorial labels drive a taxonomy-value distribution) and a **WS2-T4** `other`-bucket input (the size of the AIAAIC `other` bucket is an artifact of which AIAAIC ethical labels happen to contain matched keywords).

- **(iv) The 2026 mappings are CONSERVATIVE, not complete — OWASP widened four entries and no incident was re-classified into the widened scope. (OWASP 2026 migration, 2026-08-17.)** The migration renumbered codes and deliberately stopped there. The 2026 edition widened: **`LLM08` Hidden Context Exposure** (from the system prompt specifically to *all* non-user-facing context — developer instructions, retrieved policy text, tool/function schemas), **`LLM01`** (cross-modal injection hidden in image/audio), **`LLM04`** (model-artifact provenance failure), **`LLM05`** (fine-tuning subversion), **`LLM10`** (insecure code generated by assistants at scale). Incidents that would newly qualify were **not** added, because that is re-running classifiers over `description` — the exact coupling that silently relabelled 372 AIAAIC entries in the WS0-T3 cascade. **Consequence for WS2-T3 (`{method, confidence}`): absence of a widened-scope code means "never assessed under 2026 scope," NOT "assessed and excluded"** — same trap as (iii)'s 708 AIRI rows, and any confidence model reading absence as a negative signal will be wrong here too. **WS2-T2's annotator study is the right instrument**; the widened entries are the natural stratum to sample. Source: `docs/audits/owasp-llm-2026-migration-delta-2026-08-17.md` §6.
- **(v) `mitre_atlas` and `nist_ai_rmf` are derived from HAND-WRITTEN heuristic tables, and OWASP now publishes authoritative replacements. (Surfaced 2026-08-17 while renumbering those tables.)** `merge_and_dedupe.py`'s `LLM_TO_ATLAS` / `LLM_TO_NIST` and `parse_existing.py`'s `LLM_TO_ATLAS` map each OWASP code to one or two ATLAS techniques / NIST subcategories by author judgment, and `fill_taxonomy()` cascades those onto entries that arrive without them — so **a large share of the corpus's ATLAS and NIST mappings are downstream of roughly 30 hand-authored table cells.** The 2026 release ships machine-readable crosswalks for exactly these at `GenAI-LLM-Top10/2026/final/mappings/`: `mitre-atlas-2026.06.json`, `nist-ai-rmf-1.0.json`, `cwe-4.20.json`, `asi-2026.json`, `dsgai-v1.0.json` (note ATLAS `2026.06` matches the version already pinned in `mappings/mitre_atlas.json`). **This is an ACCURACY change, not a renumbering** — it will move real mappings on real rows, so it needs the field-level delta gate and a label-scientist owner, and must not be smuggled into a refresh. Flagged now because the tables were just touched and the provenance is fresh; **the heuristics are currently the weakest link in the mapping chain and nothing on any surface says so.**

## Load-bearing precedents (findable without archaeology — user-directed 2026-07-29)
- **PRECEDENT 4 — the set of build INPUTS includes the previous `data/incidents.json`, and a totals check will not tell you when you have missed it. (OWASP 2026 migration, 2026-08-17. The general half — *checks that cannot fail; the counterweight is a different route* — was promoted to CLAUDE.md working agreement 6 on 2026-08-18, after this instance showed Phase 1's dominant failure mode recurring post-phase in an unrelated tool; the retain-on-drop half below stays here because it is specific to this pipeline.)** `merge_and_dedupe.py`'s retain-on-drop step (`load_retained_priors()`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-01-retain-on-drop-design.md`) carries entries that every source has dropped **VERBATIM out of the previous `data/incidents.json`**, so the never-delete invariant holds. **Those rows are not re-derived from any ingest feed.** A migration that rewrote every `owasp_llm` array in `legacy/` + `ingest/` and then rebuilt therefore **missed 9 retained rows (16 assignments), which kept 2025 codes and would have kept them through every future rebuild.** **The diagnostic point, and the reason this is a precedent and not a bug report: the TOTAL was still exactly 17,498 — correct — while the DISTRIBUTION was wrong** (LLM03 +9 / LLM04 −9, LLM05 +7 / LLM10 −7). A count check, a schema validation, and the full test suite all passed on the wrong corpus. **What caught it was a CONTROL BUILD** — a clean worktree at `HEAD` rebuilt with the pre-migration pipeline and inputs, compared entry-by-entry against the crosswalk image — i.e. the same *different-route* counterweight Phase 1 recorded as the answer to checks-that-cannot-fail. **General rule for any future field-level migration here: the input set is `legacy/` + `ingest/` + the previous `data/incidents.json`, and correctness is established per-entry against a control build, never by a total.** Note this does **not** relax "never hand-edit `data/*.json`" (PRECEDENT 2's test still applies — scripts write it, so hands off): the retention source was migrated **by a deterministic, delta-verified script, before the rebuild**, which is the sanctioned route rather than an exception to the rule.
- **PRECEDENT 3 — "IDLE IS NOT DONE," and repeated idle-without-progress late in a long context is a REPLACE signal, not a re-prompt signal. (User-directed 2026-07-29; promoted to CLAUDE.md working agreement 3 so a future session inherits it as the default rather than rediscovering it.)** **The rate is the finding: four occurrences across three agents in ONE session.** (i) The original WS0-T4 conduct-half specialist died with the entire deliverable uncommitted and the suite RED — the foreman found it only by reading the tree at resume. (ii)+(iii) `review-ws0t4-conduct` went idle **twice** without sending its verdict and delivered a complete, high-quality review only when asked for it directly — matching the board's older note that gates do this on every review so far. (iv) `ws0t4-conduct-2` went idle **mid-task with the D22 register unstarted and the tree clean**, its idle summary describing the PREVIOUS deliverable (the bounce-#1 clearance, already accepted). **The verification that catches this: grep for the vocabulary the task would have introduced, not for errors.** Case (iv) was caught by `Select-String` for `register`/`same PR`/`git clone`/`accounting` in `docs/INGESTION_CONDUCT.md` returning exactly one hit — the OLD `## What is out of scope for this module` heading — plus no commits past `7b32a3f8`. A clean tree and a confident summary are **indistinguishable from success** if you only check for breakage; the distinguishing content is the only reliable signal. **Corollary the user set, and the reasoning generalises: late-context agents degrade in exactly this shape** — idle mid-task, summarizing past work instead of doing new work (the instance in case (iv) was at ~450k tokens). **When remaining scope is small and well-defined, KILL and dispatch fresh rather than reminding: a clean context executing a clear brief beats a saturated one being reminded.** Re-prompt at most once, then replace. **Standing consequence for the foreman: treat "reported complete" and "actually complete" as two separate facts, and establish the second by reading the repo** — which is also why the committed-artifact rule (agreement 1) and this one reinforce each other: an uncommitted deliverable is one session boundary away from never having existed, and E18 plus E20 are five and two more instances of exactly that. **⚠ ADDENDUM 2026-07-29 — THE CHECK IS LOCAL, and a plausible misdiagnosis of it must be pre-empted, because it would degrade the check that caught case (iv).** The specialist in case (iv) later attributed its false "done" signal to **not having pushed the branch to `origin`** — "everything sat in local commits only, invisible to any check that reads the remote" — and proposed push-after-every-batch as the lesson. **The push is good practice and was worth doing** (unpushed work is one crash from gone; this project has lost deliverables that way), **but it is NOT the root cause, and the timestamps settle it.** Every foreman check was local — `git log --oneline main..HEAD`, `Select-String` on the working file, `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` — **none read `origin`**, so push state could not have affected the result. Timeline: `7b32a3f8` (bounce-fix docs) committed **19:54:12**; the agent went idle **19:55:05**, about a minute later, with the register unstarted; the foreman's check correctly found nothing past `7b32a3f8`; the register was then built at `0bdfc6eb` **23:03:57**, over three hours later and **after the prompt**. The work was not done-but-invisible — **it had not started.** **Why the misdiagnosis is actively harmful if recorded: it implies the verification should read the REMOTE, which is strictly worse** — the remote lags local by definition, so committed-but-unpushed work would read as absent and produce exactly the false negative the agreement exists to prevent. **Keep the two lessons separate: (1) push after every commit batch, for durability against crashes — real, and unrelated to detection; (2) do not go idle mid-task, and never let an idle summary describe a PRIOR deliverable — that is the actual failure.** General form, worth more than the instance: **when an agent volunteers a root cause for its own failure, verify the causal claim with the same rigor as any other finding** — a self-diagnosis is a claim, and this one was confidently wrong about a mechanism the agent could not observe. (Note: this precedent's own quotation of the old `## What is out of scope for this module` heading is a record of what a grep returned at a point in time — accurate as history, not stale text.)
- **PRECEDENT 1 — "derive it at build time" can INDUSTRIALISE the staleness it means to prevent. (D8 marking half, 2026-07-29.)** The foreman objected to a hand-authored `data/source_freshness.json` and pushed for a build-time generator. **schema-architect inverted the argument and was right.** The authoritative source-health counter lives on the `refresh-state` branch and **reading it requires network, which the build path forbids** — so a generator could only ever read `main`'s copy, which **D5 leaves stale BY DESIGN** (its explicitly-accepted intended divergence). A generator would therefore stamp that staleness into a published artifact **automatically, on every build**, converting an occasional manual error into a systematic one. **The curated file is the safer artifact, not the riskier one**, provided it dates itself and is checked. Several of its fields are also **underivable in principle**: `stale_since: 2026-06-07` comes from the WS4-T9 run-log audit, not from a counter that keeps only a tally; the `hold` block records D8's decision and its 2026-08-28 deadline; `coverage` states what nothing measures. **The general rule: before demanding a value be derived rather than curated, establish that the deriving process can actually SEE the truth. If it can only see a known-stale proxy, derivation automates the lie.** What was built instead is stronger than either option the foreman weighed: the registry **declares which copy it read** (`observed_from`), and `check_registry_provenance()` in `scripts/validate.py` holds it to that claim — foreman-verified firing (a forged `status: ok` → exit 1 naming the discrepancy; restored → exit 0). Honestly bounded, and documented as bounded: passing means the registry matches the copy it says it read, **not** that it is current. Currency is the weekly reconciliation (spec §6b), which must sit between the restore and persist steps of `auto-refresh.yml` — the only window in which the authoritative counter is in the tree.
- **PRECEDENT 2 — the scope of "never hand-edit `data/*.json`". It protects GENERATED OUTPUTS from being clobbered; it is not a blanket ban on hand-authored files under `data/`.** The foreman read it as unconditional and was wrong. **`data/curation_overrides.json` is the standing counter-example**: 53 KB, hand-maintained by humans across multiple PRs (#85/#91/#92), read by `merge_and_dedupe.py:905/947/1382`, and **written by zero scripts** — its own `_doc` says "Add entries here as incidents are reviewed." **A file nothing writes cannot be clobbered**, which is the harm the rule exists to prevent. **This does NOT relax the rule where it bites:** `data/incidents.json`, `data/stats.json`, the three `incidents.min.json` mirrors and every other build output remain absolutely off-limits to hand editing — those ARE regenerated, and a hand edit there is silently destroyed on the next build. **Test to apply: does any script write this file? If yes, never touch it by hand. If no, it is source, and it lives wherever it is most useful to readers.** Note the corollary schema-architect argued and the foreman accepted: burying such a file outside `data/` to satisfy a misread rule would keep the corpus's own freshness statement out of what ships — a smaller version of the dishonesty being fixed.

It retracted that one tool call later, after the logs showed "Restored …". It is recorded here because a chat-only retraction is exactly the kind of record agreement 1 says does not survive.

Fence vs constant **byte-identical by sha256** (`f232eca4…`).
